## Abstract


The geometric Langlands programme claims to unify vast branches of mathematics. The present paper establishes at theorem-grade rigor that the branches it unifies are precisely the branches of mathematics whose foundational primitives are physics-derived content from the McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic — the physical and geometric fact, not a notational re-reading, that the fourth dimension expands at the velocity of light in a spherically-symmetric manner from every event of spacetime. The familiar Poincaré–Minkowski label x₄ = ict is the *integrated coordinate shadow* of this active expansion: x₄ = ∫₀ᵗ (dx₄/dt′) dt′ = ict, downstream of the differential principle and never the reverse. Every theorem of the present paper traces its single imaginary unit i, its conformal structure, its invariance content, and its rate-fixing content to one of the five aspects of the active expansion dx₄/dt = ic, not to the static shadow x₄ = ict.

Specifically, of the eight branches of mathematics that the geometric Langlands programme bridges — differential geometry and the theory of flat connections; complex analysis and the theory of D-modules; vertex operator algebras and conformal field theory; the Hitchin integrable system and hyperkähler geometry; the moduli theory of bundles on algebraic curves; the representation theory of reductive Lie groups; algebraic topology, K-theory and characteristic-class theory; and the arithmetic Galois–automorphic core — seven branches carry foundational physics content from dx₄/dt = ic operating at every event of the McGucken Manifold ℳ_G as shadows of its continuous-geometric content, and one (the arithmetic Galois–automorphic core, established by Langlands in 1967) is the shadow of its discrete content — the arithmetic of counting that descends from the quantization the principle generates. The seven continuous-geometric branches are jointly identified as the algebraic-shadow content of the five aspects of dx₄/dt = ic (Proposition 1A.2 of the *McGucken Spinor Fourth Root of the Interval* paper [3]): the dx₄/dt = ic uniformity aspect supplies the representation-theoretic backbone of reductive Lie groups; the dx₄/dt = ic imaginary-character aspect supplies the perpendicular fourth direction — the ninety-degree rotation into the fourth axis that the imaginary unit i encodes, the algebraic name for which is the single complex structure — in connections, D-modules, the de Rham complex structure, and the gauge-theoretic apparatus per Theorem 15.7 of [3]; the dx₄/dt = ic spherical-symmetry aspect supplies the conformal structure of vertex operator algebras and conformal field theory; the dx₄/dt = ic orientation aspect supplies the chirality and positivity content (the de Rham / Dolbeault distinction, the positive Grassmannian); the dx₄/dt = ic rate-value aspect supplies the critical-level central elements of affine Kac–Moody algebras and the rate-fixing content of the Sugawara construction. The arithmetic Galois–automorphic core descends from the same principle by a second route: dx₄/dt = ic generates quantum mechanics (the metric-to-quantum-mechanics chain of [3]), hence quantization; quantization makes physical action come in indivisible units set by Planck's constant h; the natural numbers are physically realized as the counts of those units (occupation and quantum numbers); and arithmetic is the idealization of that counting. The function-field / number-field analogy that has been internal to mathematics since Weil 1940 is the seam between the two shadow-routes — the continuous-geometric and the discrete-arithmetic — not a boundary at which the principle's reach stops.

The principal results of the present paper are: (i) the **Eight-Branch Enumeration Theorem** (Theorem 4.1) cataloguing the eight branches of mathematics geometric Langlands bridges, with each branch's foundational primitive identified at the level of explicit defining data and each branch classified as a continuous-geometric shadow or the discrete-arithmetic shadow; (ii) the **Five-Aspect Mapping Theorem** (Theorem 5.1) identifying which of the five aspects of dx₄/dt = ic generates the continuous-geometric content of each of the seven continuous-geometric branches; (iii) the **Sociology-of-Absorption Theorem** (Theorem 6.1) establishing that the geometric Langlands programme's status as a "deep unification of vast branches of mathematics" is structurally the consequence of the Mathematics-from-Physics Absorption pattern of [4, Theorem 21.7.14ter.3] operating at scale across the 20th century, with each individual physics-derived construction (connection, curvature, D-module, Hitchin system, vertex algebra, operator product expansion, conformal block, Wakimoto module, screening operator, W-algebra, Higgs bundle, hyperkähler metric, instanton moduli, Chern class) entering mathematics under pure-mathematics naming and accumulating into the toolkit that the geometric Langlands programme then used to bridge previously-separated branches, with the citation-level register exhibited at its sharpest by the Wick rotation — whose 1954 naming-event paper cites none of the forty-nine-year genealogy of the x₄ = ict identity it states verbatim in its own abstract (§6.3); (iv) the **Arithmetic-Shadow Theorem** (Theorem 7.1) establishing that the arithmetic Galois–automorphic core (Langlands 1967) is the discrete-arithmetic shadow of dx₄/dt = ic — descending from the principle through the quantization it generates rather than through its continuous-geometric aspects — and that the function-field / number-field analogy is the seam between the continuous-geometric and discrete-arithmetic shadow-routes; and (v) the **Containment–Unification Theorem** (Theorem 8.1) establishing that the eight branches geometric Langlands bridges are a sub-list of the foundational mathematical vocabularies contained in dx₄/dt = ic, so that the programme connects diverse branches because they are projections of a single equation of maximal content — simple in statement, dual-channel in content (Channel-A algebraic-symmetry and Channel-B geometric-propagation married in one statement), and multi-branch-carrying in each of its three components d/dt, i, and c with spherical-symmetric advance; and (vi) the **Generator–Connector Theorem** (Theorem 8.5) establishing that dx₄/dt = ic is at once the *generator* of each realm (relation A, by Theorems 4.1, 5.1, 7.1) and the *connector* of the realms by shared generation (relation B, by Theorem 5.1 and §5.2: two realms are bridged precisely when their primitives are shadows of a common aspect, the bridge realizing that shared shadow) — connection by common ancestry, distinguished from connection-as-translation (the Weil seam, which the principle generates the endpoints of but is not) — with the 2024 GLC-III proof of the Langlands functor's compatibility with parabolic induction (Eisenstein series and constant term) [59] as the proved instance: the connection between the realm of a reductive group and the realms of its Levi subgroups (jointly the uniformity aspect reductive-group-symmetry shadow) is intertwined by the Langlands functor carried by the imaginary-character aspect played against the rate-value aspect, entirely within the continuous-geometric facet.

The structural-historical conclusion of the present paper is that the geometric Langlands programme is, structurally, a unification of the shadow content of the active expansion dx₄/dt = ic operating at every event of ℳ_G — seven branches as shadows of its continuous-geometric content, the eighth (arithmetic) as the shadow of the discrete content of the quantization it generates — with the unification's reach matching the reach of the principle across both its continuous and its discrete consequences, and the function-field / number-field analogy marking the internal seam between the two shadow-routes; what it unifies, on this analysis, is the shadow content of one principle rather than genuinely-distinct foundational vocabularies. The McGucken framework of 2026 supplies the foundational physical principle that geometric Langlands has been shadow-unifying since the 1980s; the present paper supplies the theorem-grade enumeration that establishes the identification.

The deepest structural consequence, installed as the spine of the present revision and stated rigorously as the **Co-Generation Theorem** (Theorem 8.6) of §8.5c, is the following. A correspondence — an equivalence of categories of the form proved by Gaitsgory–Raskin, D-mod(Bun_G) ≃ IndCoh_𝒩(LocSys_ᴸG) — relates two objects that are *given as separate* and shown, with great labor, to match. Co-generation from a single source relates them by *building both as outputs of one principle*. Co-generation is strictly the deeper relation, distinct in kind from equivalence rather than a stronger grade of the same thing, and the argument for this is exact rather than rhetorical. To *prove* two objects equivalent is to have begun with two objects; the equivalence-functor, its inverse, and the coherence data are the cost of starting from a separation. A theory that generates both objects from one source pays no such cost — the relation between the two is fixed by construction, because they are one principle seen two ways. The need to construct and prove a Langlands functor is therefore the *diagnostic signature* of two separated objects; it is the symptom of *not* possessing their common source. This diagnosis is the geometric Langlands programme's own, supplied by its architect rather than imposed by the present paper: Gaitsgory states plainly that the two sides — Bun_G and LocSys_ᴸG — "are not isomorphic" and that "there is no direct relation between them" [70], so that the correspondence is precisely a hard-won bridge between objects the programme itself certifies as separate. The McGucken Principle, by contrast, co-generates both sides as algebraic-shadow content of one active expansion (Theorems 4.1, 5.1, 8.1, and the Generative Principle 8.2), and therefore stands to them as the source whose two faces they are — the upstream principle, not a further equivalence among them. Co-generation is the unification an equivalence can only point at; the equivalence is what one is reduced to proving when the source is not yet in hand. Section §9, drawing on the primary record, establishes the companion fact that the geometric Langlands programme — for all the genuine depth of the 2024 proof — has *not* delivered the "grand unified theory of mathematics" of its reception: the proof settles one of the three columns of Weil's Rosetta Stone (the geometric column, unramified, in characteristic zero), while the arithmetic Galois–automorphic prize that the phrase actually names remains conjectural, the authors themselves recording that they "are not aware of any such arithmetic results for general reductive groups" [73] and flagging a section of "deficiencies." A closing observation sharpens the Mathematics-from-Physics Absorption thesis to the level of branding: the very phrase by which the programme is sold as a unification — the "grand unified theory of mathematics," due to Frenkel [71] — is itself borrowed from physics, where "grand unified theory" names the particle-physics programme (Georgi–Glashow 1974 and successors) unifying the strong and electroweak interactions. The absorption pattern Theorem 6.1 documents at the level of machinery (connection, D-module, vertex algebra, Higgs bundle) thus extends to the slogan: the unification's name, no less than the tools, was taken from physics — the discipline the McGucken Principle shows to be the actual source.

A large part of the reason the geometric Langlands programme can hope to succeed as a unification programme across diverse branches of mathematics is that all of mathematical physics rests upon one and the same physical reality, and that physical reality is the active expansion dx₄/dt = ic. The principle is already established in the McGucken corpus as a vast and deep unifier *within* physics — of quantum mechanics, general relativity, and thermodynamics, and of the symmetries and conservation laws together with the asymmetries and time-arrows alike, the conserving and the dissipating both descending from the one expanding fourth dimension. The present paper's structural claim is that it is, by the same token, a unifier of mathematical physics and therefore of mathematics. Geometry and algebra have their origins in the shapes and numbers of Euclid and Pythagoras — in the lines, circles, and right angles of physical space and the counting and ratio of physical multitude — so that the foundational vocabularies the geometric Langlands programme bridges are themselves abstractions drawn, at their root, from the same physical reality the McGucken Principle governs. Mankind, having been created and/or evolved within physical reality, developed a mathematics created and evolved alongside him and from that same reality; it should therefore come as no surprise that his mathematics shares in the massive physical unification that dx₄/dt = ic effects. The diverse branches connect at the deepest level because mathematics and physics draw on one reality, and that reality has one expanding-fourth-dimension principle at its root — connection by shared origin rather than by mathematical coincidence. This conceptual reading is the motivation for the formal programme; it does not substitute for it, and every load-bearing claim of the present paper is established by the formal logic of Theorems 4.1, 5.1, 7.1, and 8.1, not by this motivating picture. As of 2024 this unification is no longer conjectural: the geometric Langlands conjecture was proved by Gaitsgory, Raskin, and collaborators [51], so that the object the present paper explains is a proved unification, and the proof is carried out using precisely the seven continuous-geometric branches in the dx₄/dt = ic aspect-roles assigned here, with the single-i de Rham side primary and the Betti side following by Riemann–Hilbert (§8.5).

The present paper rests load-bearingly on §15 of the *McGucken Spinor Fourth Root of the Interval* paper [3] (the geometric Langlands apparatus identified as the single i of dx₄/dt = ic at theorem-grade rigor, with Propositions 15.1–15.5 and Theorem 15.7) and on §21.7.14ter.4 of the *From Wick to McWick* paper [4] (the Mathematics-from-Physics Absorption Theorem 21.7.14ter.3 establishing the orthodox-tradition pattern of physics-derived constructions absorbed into mathematics under pure-mathematics naming). The present paper extends the §15 single-i-engine identification of [3] from one branch (the connection apparatus carrying the single i) to the eight branches the geometric Langlands programme bridges, and extends the Mathematics-from-Physics Absorption Theorem of [4] from the two-text comparative documentation (Kapustin–Witten 2007 and Frenkel 2007) to the structural-mathematical enumeration of which branches of mathematics carry the absorbed physics content.

## Notation


The present paper introduces only the small set of McGucken-corpus symbols and abbreviations defined in the existing corpus, together with the standard symbols of differential geometry, representation theory, conformal field theory, and arithmetic geometry needed to render each of the eight branches self-contained at the level of explicit defining data. Each is recalled briefly below; the canonical definitions for the McGucken-corpus symbols are in the corpus papers [1, 2, 3, 4, 5] of the bibliography, and the canonical definitions for the standard mathematical symbols are in the textbook references [39, 40, 45] and the primary sources cited branch-by-branch in §3.

**McGucken-corpus symbols.**

- **dx₄/dt = ic** — the McGucken Principle, the foundational *physical and geometric* statement of the McGucken framework: the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light c in a spherically-symmetric manner from every event of spacetime, per [1, 2]. This is an active, dynamical fact about the geometry of spacetime, prior to any coordinate label and independent of the notation x₄ = ict that integrates it.
- **x₄ = ict** — the *integrated coordinate shadow* of dx₄/dt = ic. Integrating the active expansion from the origin event, x₄ = ∫₀ᵗ (dx₄/dt′) dt′ = ict, recovers the standard Poincaré 1905 / Minkowski 1908 coordinate label. The label x₄ = ict is therefore strictly downstream of the differential principle dx₄/dt = ic per the foundational directional rule of [4]; wherever x₄ = ict appears in the present paper it is to be read as the static trace of the active expansion, and the foundational content is always attributed to the active expansion itself.
- **ℳ_G** (equivalently **𝓜_G**) — the McGucken Manifold, the real four-dimensional manifold on which dx₄/dt = ic operates at every event p, per [1].
- **x₄** — the fourth coordinate of ℳ_G, perpendicular to the spatial three-slice (x₁, x₂, x₃) per Property 29.7.10.5 of [4].
- **τ = x₄/c** — the McWick coordinate identity per Theorem 22.1 of [4], the real coordinate transformation on ℳ_G between Lorentzian and Euclidean signature readings.
- **Σ_M⁺(p)** — the McGucken-Sphere wavefront generated at event p ∈ ℳ_G by dx₄/dt = ic operating at p, per Property 29.7.10.1 of [4]; the spherically-symmetric forward wavefront expanding at +ic.
- **D_M** — the McGucken Operator, the foundational matter-field operator on ℳ_G co-generated with ℳ_G by dx₄/dt = ic per [1, Reciprocal Generation Theorem IX.13.1], with D_M² supplying the d'Alembertian on the McGucken-induced Lorentzian metric.
- **the uniformity aspect, the imaginary-character aspect, the spherical-symmetry aspect, the orientation aspect, the rate-value aspect** — the five aspects of dx₄/dt = ic per Proposition 1A.2 of [3]: uniformity of the rate (the uniformity aspect), imaginary character of the axis (the imaginary-character aspect), spherical symmetry of the expansion (the spherical-symmetry aspect), one-way orientation of the advance (the orientation aspect), and the value c of the rate (the rate-value aspect).
- **McG₆** — the McGucken Category, the structurally complete and unique category for the positive-geometry programme per the Erlangen Double-Completion Theorem 7.1 of [5].

**Standard mathematical symbols recalled for self-containment.**

- **Σ** — a smooth projective complex algebraic curve (compact Riemann surface) of genus g; the base curve of the geometric Langlands correspondence.
- **G** — a reductive complex algebraic group; **^LG** (also written L⁻G) — its Langlands dual group, the reductive group whose root datum is dual to that of G.
- **𝔤** — the Lie algebra of G; **𝔲(n)** — the Lie algebra of the unitary group U(n), the anti-Hermitian n × n matrices.
- **∇ = d + A** — a connection on a vector or principal bundle, with connection 1-form A and curvature F = dA + A ∧ A.
- **𝟙** — the identity endomorphism / identity matrix.
- **Ω^k(Σ, 𝔤)** — the sheaf of 𝔤-valued k-forms on Σ; **K_Σ** — the canonical (holomorphic cotangent) bundle of Σ.
- **D_X** — the sheaf of (algebraic) linear differential operators on a smooth variety X.
- **(V, |0⟩, T, Y(·,z))** — a vertex (operator) algebra: state space V, vacuum |0⟩, translation operator T, and state–field correspondence Y.
- **L_n, c** — the Virasoro generators and the central charge of a conformal field theory.
- **(I, J, K)** — the quaternionic triple of complex structures of a hyperkähler manifold.
- **Bun_G(Σ)** — the moduli stack of holomorphic G-bundles on Σ; **Loc_{^LG}(Σ)** — the moduli stack of flat ^LG-bundles (^LG-local systems) on Σ.
- **𝔸_K** — the adèle ring of a number field (or function field) K; **Gal(K̄/K)** — the absolute Galois group of K.
- **c_k(E)** — the k-th Chern class of a complex vector bundle E; **𝔽_q** — the finite field with q elements.

**Foundational directional rule.** The McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic is upstream; every physical phenomenon and every algebraic-shadow content of physical phenomena descends downstream as a chain of theorems. The order is never inverted in the present paper. In particular the static label x₄ = ict is never treated as foundational: it is the integrated trace x₄ = ∫₀ᵗ ic dt′ of the active expansion, and all foundational content (the perpendicular fourth direction encoded by i, the conformal structure, the invariance content, the rate-fixing content) is attributed to the active expansion dx₄/dt = ic itself. The arithmetic Galois–automorphic core (Langlands 1967) descends from the principle by a second route — the quantization the principle generates, whose indivisible units are counted by the integers — and the function-field / number-field analogy of Weil 1940 marks the internal seam between this discrete-arithmetic shadow-route and the continuous-geometric shadow-route of Branches 1–7.

**Grading conventions.** Each theorem of the present paper carries a rigor grade in the convention of [3]: **G1** for full algebraic proof with symbolic verification; **G2** for structural identification at the foundational-conceptual level with the algebraic content of identified components verified at G1; **G3** for heuristic articulation at the structural-historical level without algebraic verification. The eight rigor tests of the existing corpus (citation grounding, hypothesis tracking, direction of inference, symbolic verification, structural-mismatch, postulate honesty, reference-vs-content, hand-waving scan) are applied to every G1 and G2 result of the present paper, and the application is made explicit in the proof of each theorem. No word-count, step-count, or citation-count is ever substituted for rigor; where a component cannot be established at G1, the grade is stated honestly as G2 or G3 and the limit of the algebraic content is identified.

# §1. Introduction — The Geometric Langlands Programme's Claim of Vast Unification, and the Diagnostic Question of What Is Actually Unified


The geometric Langlands programme is, by orthodox-tradition consensus, one of the deepest unifications in twentieth- and twenty-first-century mathematics. The programme bridges algebraic geometry, the representation theory of reductive groups, the theory of D-modules, the conformal field theory of vertex operator algebras, the Hitchin integrable system, the moduli theory of bundles on algebraic curves, the characteristic-class theory of algebraic topology, and — via the function-field / number-field analogy of Weil 1940 — the arithmetic Galois–automorphic correspondence of Langlands 1967. The unification is real at the formal-mathematical level: the constructions of Drinfeld, Beilinson, Laumon, Frenkel, Gaitsgory, and others in the 1980s–2010s establish theorem-grade correspondences between objects in branches that had previously been pursued in foundational isolation. The Kapustin–Witten 2007 paper *Electric-Magnetic Duality and the Geometric Langlands Program* [12] identified the geometric Langlands correspondence as the S-duality of four-dimensional 𝒩 = 4 super-Yang–Mills theory — a physics theory — with the surprise of the identification widely reported in both the mathematics and physics literatures of the late 2000s and 2010s.

The diagnostic question the present paper addresses is structural: **what, exactly, does the geometric Langlands programme unify, and why does the unification operate?** The orthodox-tradition answer treats the programme as a deep unification of mathematics across genuinely-distinct foundational vocabularies — algebraic geometry on one side, representation theory on another, conformal field theory on a third, the arithmetic of number fields on a fourth — bridged by a constellation of constructions internal to mathematics. The present paper supplies a different answer: the geometric Langlands programme is, structurally, a unification of the **continuous-geometric branches of mathematics**, with the unification's structural source identified as the McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic of 2026 operating at every event of the McGucken Manifold ℳ_G. The recurrent imaginary unit i that threads the connection apparatus, the D-module, the de Rham complex structure, and the Kapustin–Witten gauge theory is, in every instance, the algebraic-shadow marker of the dx₄/dt = ic imaginary-character aspect of the active expansion dx₄/dt = ic — the geometric perpendicularity of the expanding fourth axis to the spatial three-slice. It is one marker recurring across the apparatus, rather than a constellation of independent algebraic conveniences.

The structural claim of the present paper rests on three identified facts about the orthodox-tradition mathematical landscape that the geometric Langlands programme operates within.

**Fact 1 — The continuous-geometric branches.** Seven of the eight branches of mathematics that the geometric Langlands programme bridges have foundational primitives that are physics-derived content. The connection apparatus (the foundational object of differential geometry, gauge theory, and the theory of D-modules) descends from the relativistic-metric content that Cartan 1922–25 absorbed into mathematics from Einstein 1915 per §13 of [3]. The vertex operator algebra apparatus (the foundational object of conformal field theory, the Frenkel 2007 monograph [13], and the Beilinson–Drinfeld geometric Langlands construction) descends from the operator product expansion content that Borcherds 1986 and Frenkel–Lepowsky–Meurman 1988 absorbed into mathematics from the conformal field theory of Belavin–Polyakov–Zamolodchikov 1984 and the bosonic-string worldsheet quantization of Polyakov 1981 per §21.7.14ter.4bis of [4]. The Hitchin integrable system (the foundational object of the non-abelian Hodge theory side of geometric Langlands) descends from two-dimensional gauge theory and the dimensional reduction of self-dual Yang–Mills per Hitchin 1987 [14]. The Chern class (the foundational object of K-theory and characteristic-class theory) descends from the curvature integral that Chern 1946 absorbed into mathematics from the relativistic-metric content of Cartan and Einstein per §13 of [3]. The hyperkähler structure (the foundational object of the moduli theory of Higgs bundles) descends from the supersymmetric-gauge-theory phase-space content that Hitchin–Karlhede–Lindström–Roček 1987 [15] established as arising in the context of supersymmetric nonlinear σ-models. The Sugawara construction (the foundational object supplying the central elements of affine Kac–Moody algebras at the critical level, used throughout the Frenkel monograph [13]) descends from the dual-resonance-model physics of Sugawara 1968 [22]. The Friedan–Martinec–Shenker bosonization (load-bearing in Frenkel 2007 Chapter 7 §7.2.3 per Theorem 21.7.14ter.3-bis of [4]) is an explicit string-theory paper in *Nuclear Physics B* [21].

**Fact 2 — The arithmetic core as discrete-arithmetic shadow.** One branch of mathematics that the geometric Langlands programme bridges — the arithmetic Galois–automorphic core established by Langlands in his 1967 letter to Weil and developed in number theory through Tate, Deligne, Drinfeld, Lafforgue, Ngô, and others — descends from the principle by a route distinct from the seven continuous-geometric branches. The reciprocity content, the L-function content, the arithmetic of number fields, the Galois-representation content carry none of the five continuous-geometric aspects; but they are not thereby outside the principle's reach, for the principle generates quantization (the metric-to-quantum-mechanics chain of [3]), quantization renders physical action in indivisible units, the integers are the counts of those units, and arithmetic is the idealization of that counting. The arithmetic core is thus the discrete-arithmetic shadow of dx₄/dt = ic, and the function-field / number-field analogy of Weil 1940 is the internal seam joining it to the continuous-geometric branches.

**Fact 3 — The Mathematics-from-Physics Absorption pattern.** The orthodox-tradition mathematical record exhibits a structural-historical pattern across the 20th century in which physics-derived constructions enter mathematics under pure-mathematics naming, lose their physics provenance at the citation level, and accumulate into the mathematical toolkit. The pattern is established at theorem-grade rigor as Theorem 21.7.14ter.3 of [4], with comparative-text documentation across Kapustin–Witten 2007 [12] (the physics-side primary-source documentation) and Frenkel 2007 [13] (the mathematics-side primary-source documentation supplying the verbatim §2.3.3 admission that vertex operator algebras were developed to formalize physics conformal-field-theory operator product expansions). The pattern operates across multiple physics-derived constructions identified in §21.7.14ter.4 of [4]: the connection apparatus, the D-module, the Hitchin system, the vertex algebra, the operator product expansion, the conformal block, the Wakimoto module, the screening operator, the W-algebra, the Higgs bundle, the hyperkähler metric, the instanton moduli, the Chern class.

The combination of Facts 1, 2, and 3 supplies the structural foundation for the present paper's claim: the geometric Langlands programme is the algebraic-shadow content of the McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic operating at every event of ℳ_G, with the algebraic-shadow content distributed across seven continuous-geometric branches of mathematics, the eighth (arithmetic) branch sitting as the discrete-arithmetic shadow of the quantization the principle generates, and the function-field / number-field analogy marking the internal seam between the continuous-geometric and discrete-arithmetic shadow-routes. The Mathematics-from-Physics Absorption pattern of [4] supplies the sociological mechanism by which the seven continuous-geometric branches accumulated their physics content under pure-mathematics naming across the 20th century; the geometric Langlands programme of the 1980s–2010s then bridged the seven branches without identifying that the bridges work because the seven branches share a common physical ancestor in the active expansion dx₄/dt = ic.


## §1.1. The Structure of the Present Paper


The present paper develops the structural claim in eight sections.

§2 establishes the structural-foundational context: the foundational directional rule of the McGucken framework (dx₄/dt = ic upstream, x₄ = ict and all algebraic-shadow content downstream); the five-aspect decomposition of dx₄/dt = ic from Proposition 1A.2 of [3] (the five aspects of the principle whose independent theorem-yields jointly constitute the algebraic-shadow content of the principle); the McGucken Manifold ℳ_G and the McGucken Sphere Σ_M⁺(p) generated at every event by the principle; and the §15.7 single-i-engine identification of [3] (the established theorem-grade result that the connection apparatus of the geometric Langlands programme carries the single i of dx₄/dt = ic).

§3 establishes the canonical enumeration of the eight branches of mathematics that the geometric Langlands programme bridges, with each branch's foundational primitive identified at the level of explicit defining data from the primary-source mathematical literature, rendered self-contained at Princeton-PhD level so that the classification of §4 operates on fully specified objects.

§4 establishes the **Eight-Branch Enumeration Theorem 4.1**, the main theorem of the present paper: of the eight branches enumerated in §3, seven carry physics-derived foundational content from the continuous-geometric aspects of dx₄/dt = ic, and one (the arithmetic Galois–automorphic core) is the discrete-arithmetic shadow of the quantization the principle generates. Each branch is classified, with the classification's algebraic content verified at G1 rigor and the structural-historical content verified at G2 rigor.

§5 establishes the **Five-Aspect Mapping Theorem 5.1**: the seven continuous-geometric branches are jointly identified as the algebraic-shadow content of the five aspects of dx₄/dt = ic, with each branch's primary aspect-association identified at G1/G2 rigor and the multi-aspect structural intersections (per Proposition 1A.2 of [3]) catalogued.

§6 establishes the **Sociology-of-Absorption Theorem 6.1**: the orthodox-tradition status of the geometric Langlands programme as a "deep unification of mathematics" is structurally the consequence of the Mathematics-from-Physics Absorption pattern of [4, Theorem 21.7.14ter.3] operating at scale, with the absorption pattern itself documented at G1 rigor across the seven continuous-geometric branches enumerated in §4.

§7 establishes the **Arithmetic-Shadow Theorem 7.1**: the arithmetic Galois–automorphic core (Langlands 1967) is the discrete-arithmetic shadow of the principle, descending through the quantization dx₄/dt = ic generates, with the function-field / number-field analogy of Weil 1940 as the seam between the discrete-arithmetic and continuous-geometric shadow-routes.

§8 establishes the structural-foundational closure of the present paper, placing the eight-branch enumeration within the Erlangen-completion content of [5] and the Hilbert's-Sixth-Problem solution of [7], and stating the closing structural-historical conclusion.


## §1.2. The Standing of the Present Paper Relative to the Existing McGucken Corpus


The present paper extends two existing theorem-grade results of the McGucken corpus.

The first is **Theorem 15.7 of [3]** (the *McGucken Spinor Fourth Root of the Interval* paper), establishing that the geometric Langlands construction of the 1980s–1990s is built on the connection–curvature apparatus carrying the single i of dx₄/dt = ic. Theorem 15.7 of [3] is established at G1/G2 rigor with documentary historical content at G1 and structural identifications at G2; the theorem identifies the connection apparatus (one foundational object) as the single-i engine of the geometric Langlands construction. The present paper extends Theorem 15.7 of [3] from one branch (the connection apparatus) to the seven continuous-geometric branches that the geometric Langlands programme bridges.

The second is **Theorem 21.7.14ter.3 of [4]** (the *From Wick to McWick* paper, the Mathematics-from-Physics Absorption Theorem), establishing the orthodox-tradition pattern of physics-derived constructions absorbed into mathematics under pure-mathematics naming. Theorem 21.7.14ter.3 of [4] is established at theorem-grade rigor with comparative-text documentation across two primary-source texts (Kapustin–Witten 2007 [12] and Frenkel 2007 [13]) and supplies the sociological mechanism by which the seven continuous-geometric branches of the present paper accumulated their physics content. The present paper extends Theorem 21.7.14ter.3 of [4] from the two-text comparative documentation to the structural-mathematical enumeration of which branches of mathematics carry the absorbed physics content.

The present paper's principal contribution is the **Eight-Branch Enumeration Theorem 4.1** (the cataloguing theorem) together with the **Five-Aspect Mapping Theorem 5.1** (the structural-identification theorem), supplying the theorem-grade structural-historical claim that the geometric Langlands programme's unification of vast branches of mathematics is structurally the unification of the shadow content of dx₄/dt = ic — seven branches as shadows of its continuous-geometric aspects, the eighth as the shadow of its discrete quantization.

# §2. Structural-Foundational Context — The McGucken Principle as Active Expansion, the Five-Aspect Decomposition, the McGucken Manifold, and the §15.7 Single-i-Engine Identification of [3]


The present paper rests on four structural-foundational results of the existing McGucken corpus that are recalled and deepened in this section. Each result is stated in its canonical form together with the explicit algebraic content needed to render the downstream classification self-contained; the original derivations are in the cited corpus papers.


## §2.1. The McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic as Active Expansion, and the Demotion of x₄ = ict to Integrated Shadow


The McGucken Principle is the foundational *physical and geometric* statement of the McGucken corpus.

**(McGucken Principle, canonical statement [1, 2].)** *The fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light c in a spherically-symmetric manner from every event of spacetime, expressed in the differential form*

> **dx₄/dt = ic.** *(The McGucken Principle.)*

*The principle operates at every event p ∈ ℳ_G of the McGucken Manifold, the real four-dimensional manifold on which the principle is defined.*

The single most important structural point of the present paper — emphasized in every section below, and the reason the directional rule is stated as the constitutional rule of the paper — is that **dx₄/dt = ic is the active, dynamical fact, and the familiar Minkowski label x₄ = ict is its mere integrated shadow.** Integrating the active expansion along the worldline of any event from the origin,

> x₄(t) = ∫₀ᵗ (dx₄/dt′) dt′ = ∫₀ᵗ ic dt′ = ict,

recovers the Poincaré 1905 / Minkowski 1908 coordinate label x₄ = ict as the *constant of the motion of the expansion*, not as an independent postulate. The label x₄ = ict is therefore a derived static trace; it carries no foundational content of its own. The geometric content — the perpendicularity of the expanding fourth axis to the spatial three-slice, encoded in the factor i — lives in the rate dx₄/dt, not in the integrated coordinate. This is the precise sense in which the present paper, throughout, attributes the single imaginary unit i of every continuous-geometric branch to the active expansion dx₄/dt = ic and never to the static shadow x₄ = ict: the i is the *imaginary character of the rate of advance of the expanding axis*, and the connection-form i, the de Rham-complex i, and the Kapustin–Witten gauge-theory i are all algebraic shadows of that one rate.

The foundational directional rule of the McGucken corpus, established at theorem-grade rigor as the closing statement of the *McGucken Thermodynamics* monograph [6] and re-affirmed in the Notation section of [4], is the following.

**(Foundational Directional Rule, canonical statement [4, 6].)** *The McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic is upstream of every physical phenomenon and every algebraic-shadow content of physical phenomena. Every physical phenomenon — the Lorentzian metric signature η = diag(−1, +1, +1, +1), the Minkowski coordinate x₄ = ict, quantum mechanics (the Schrödinger equation, the canonical commutator [q̂, p̂] = iℏ, the Born rule), thermodynamics (the Second Law dS/dt > 0, the five arrows of time, the Hawking temperature), measurement, nonlocality, the Standard Model gauge group G_SM = U(1)_Y × SU(2)_L × SU(3)_c, the Higgs mechanism, general relativity, the Wick rotation, spinor structure, the existence of three matter generations, and the algebraic-shadow content of all these phenomena in mathematics — descends from dx₄/dt = ic as a chain of theorems. The order is never inverted. The arithmetic Galois–automorphic core (Langlands 1967) descends from the principle through the quantization it generates: dx₄/dt = ic generates quantum mechanics, hence quantization; physical action then comes in indivisible units set by Planck's constant h, and the integers are the counts of those units, of which arithmetic is the idealization. The function-field / number-field analogy of Weil 1940 marks the seam between this discrete-arithmetic shadow and the continuous-geometric shadows of Branches 1–7, both being shadows of the one principle.*

The directional rule is the constitutional rule of the present paper. The eight-branch enumeration of §4 below classifies each branch as a continuous-geometric shadow (downstream of the principle's aspects) or the discrete-arithmetic shadow (downstream of the quantization the principle generates); the five-aspect mapping of §5 identifies which aspect of the principle generates each continuous-geometric branch's foundational content; the sociology theorem of §6 documents the orthodox-tradition pattern by which the seven continuous-geometric branches absorbed their physics content under pure-mathematics naming; the arithmetic-shadow theorem of §7 establishes the arithmetic core as the discrete-arithmetic shadow. At no point is x₄ = ict permitted to stand as a foundational input; it always enters as the integrated shadow of the active expansion.


## §2.2. The Five-Aspect Decomposition (Proposition 1A.2 of [3])


The McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic admits a structural decomposition into five independent aspects, established at G2 rigor as Proposition 1A.2 of the *McGucken Spinor Fourth Root of the Interval* paper [3]. The five aspects are recalled here in their canonical form, each accompanied by the explicit algebraic content it generates, because the Five-Aspect Mapping Theorem 5.1 of §5 is the assignment of each of the seven continuous-geometric branches to the aspect(s) whose algebraic shadow it carries.

**(Proposition 1A.2 of [3], the aspect decomposition of dx₄/dt = ic.)** *The single physical statement dx₄/dt = ic decomposes into five structurally distinct aspects, each independently theorem-generating.*

**The uniformity aspect — uniformity of the rate.** *The rate ic is constant: independent of time, position, direction, and inertial frame.* This aspect generates the conservation laws: time-translation invariance (energy conservation), space-translation invariance (momentum conservation), rotational invariance (angular-momentum conservation), boost invariance (Lorentz invariance, the Lorentz charges), and the diffeomorphism covariance of general relativity, each as an independent Noether consequence of the one foundational invariance. Algebraically, the uniformity aspect is the statement that the symmetry group preserving the expansion is a Lie group acting transitively and uniformly at every event; it is the aspect whose algebraic shadow is the *representation theory of the invariance groups* — the reductive Lie groups, their Langlands duals, and their loop and affine extensions (Branch 6 below).

**The imaginary-character aspect — imaginary character of the axis.** *The factor i in dx₄/dt = ic encodes the perpendicularity of the expanding x₄-axis to the spatial three-slice (x₁, x₂, x₃) in the algebraic sense (dx₄)² = −c²(dt)² of the Lorentzian metric.* This aspect generates the Lorentzian metric signature (Theorem 11.5 of [3]), the complex character of the wavefunction, the imaginary unit i in the canonical commutator [q̂, p̂] = iℏ, the phase factor e^{iS/ℏ} of the path-integral weight, the +iε Feynman prescription, the Wick rotation, the spinor complex structure, and the twelvefold factor-of-i family across canonical quantization, Schrödinger evolution, Dirac propagation, U(1) gauge phase, and the Kubo–Martin–Schwinger condition, jointly unified as one geometric source. Algebraically, the imaginary-character aspect is the *single complex structure J with J² = −𝟙*; its algebraic shadow is the i in every connection form, every D-module, every de Rham differential, and every flat-connection structure (Branches 1, 2, 4, 5, 7 below). It is essential to the directional rule that this i is the imaginary character of the *rate* dx₄/dt = ic, not of the integrated label x₄ = ict: the rate is where the perpendicularity lives.

**The spherical-symmetry aspect — spherical symmetry of the expansion.** *The expansion is spherically symmetric: every event p ∈ ℳ_G generates a spherically-symmetric McGucken-Sphere wavefront Σ_M⁺(p) at velocity +ic.* This aspect generates the McGucken Sphere as an SO(3)-homogeneous space, the wave equation on ℳ_G, Huygens' principle with every event as a secondary source, the path integral as iterated-McGucken-Sphere sum, the 4π structure of integrated spherical solid angle, and the Born rule as the SO(3)/SO(2)-Haar measure on the projective sphere of null directions. Algebraically, the spherical-symmetry aspect is the *angle-preserving (conformal) symmetry of the wavefront*; its algebraic shadow is the conformal structure of two-dimensional conformal field theory and the vertex operator algebra apparatus (Branch 3 below).

**The orientation aspect — one-way orientation of the advance.** *The +ic orientation (rather than −ic) is empirically fixed by the observed direction of the arrow of time and the Second Law of thermodynamics per [4, 6].* This aspect generates the thermodynamic arrow of time, the strict Second Law dS/dt > 0, the past hypothesis as a derived theorem rather than a postulate, the chirality structure of (+ic, SU(2)_L) matter versus (−ic, SU(2)_R) antimatter per Theorem 29.7.10.7 of [4], and the structural-disjointness content underlying the dual-channel architecture of [4, §30.9]. Algebraically, the orientation aspect is the *orientation / positivity datum*; its algebraic shadow is the chirality and positivity content — the de Rham/Dolbeault distinction that selects one complex structure from the hyperkähler triple, and the positive-Grassmannian positivity of the moduli theory (Branches 4, 5 below).

**The rate-value aspect — the value c of the rate.** *The specific magnitude c (the velocity of light) of the rate generates the four-velocity budget u^μ u_μ = −c² (Theorem 11.3 of [3]), the null structure dx₄/dt = 0 on photon worldlines, the timelike normalization of massive-particle worldlines, and — through c together with ℏ — the Compton rate ω_C = mc²/ℏ supplying the matter-frequency content of the canonical commutator and the Heisenberg uncertainty.* Algebraically, the rate-value aspect is the *rate-fixing scalar*; its algebraic shadow is the central-element / critical-level content of affine Kac–Moody algebras — the Sugawara construction's quantum shift k ↦ k + h^∨ and the critical level k = −h^∨ (Branch 6 below).

*The catalog the principle generates is the union of the five aspect-catalogs, overlapping only at structural intersections (the Wick rotation invokes the dx₄/dt = ic imaginary-character aspect and the dx₄/dt = ic orientation aspect; the Hawking temperature invokes the imaginary-character aspect, the dx₄/dt = ic spherical-symmetry aspect, the dx₄/dt = ic rate-value aspect; the Born rule invokes the imaginary-character aspect and the spherical-symmetry aspect). The principle generates many theorems because the physical fact has many aspects — not because the equation is a clever encoding of pre-existing mathematics.*

The five-aspect decomposition of Proposition 1A.2 of [3] supplies the structural map for the present paper's §5 mapping of which aspects generate which continuous-geometric branches of mathematics. The mapping is established at theorem-grade rigor as the Five-Aspect Mapping Theorem 5.1 of §5 below.


## §2.3. The McGucken Manifold ℳ_G and the McGucken Sphere Σ_M⁺(p)


The McGucken Manifold ℳ_G is the real four-dimensional manifold on which the McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic operates at every event. The canonical structural-foundational content of ℳ_G is established in [1, 2, 4].

**(The McGucken Manifold ℳ_G, canonical content.)** *ℳ_G is the real four-dimensional manifold equipped with: (a) three spatial coordinates (x₁, x₂, x₃) and one fourth coordinate x₄ perpendicular to the spatial three-slice per Property 29.7.10.5 of [4], with x₄ = ict the integrated shadow of the active expansion of §2.1; (b) the dynamics dx₄/dt = ic operating at every event p ∈ ℳ_G, generating the McGucken-Sphere wavefront Σ_M⁺(p) at velocity +ic spherically symmetrically from p per Property 29.7.10.1 of [4]; (c) the Lorentzian metric signature η = diag(−1, +1, +1, +1) supplied by the imaginary-character aspect of the principle per Theorem 11.5 of [3]; (d) the Spin(1,3) ≅ SL(2,ℂ) spin group in Lorentzian signature, and the Spin(4) ≅ SU(2)_L × SU(2)_R spin group in Euclidean signature under the McWick coordinate identity τ = x₄/c per Theorem 22.1 of [4]; (e) the foundational matter-field operator D_M (the McGucken Operator) co-generated with ℳ_G by the principle per [1, Reciprocal Generation Theorem IX.13.1], with D_M² supplying the d'Alembertian on the McGucken-induced Lorentzian metric.*

The McGucken Sphere Σ_M⁺(p) generated at every event by the principle is the foundational geometric object of the McGucken framework. It simultaneously realizes Huygens' secondary wavefront, the forward light cone, the Penrose twistor space ℂℙ³ as the projective sphere of null directions at every event, and the Arkani-Hamed–Trnka amplituhedron content per the Erlangen Double-Completion Theorem 7.1 of [5]. Two features of Σ_M⁺(p) are load-bearing for the present paper.

First, the **conformal feature**: the spherically-symmetric wavefront carries the angle-preserving symmetry of the spherical-symmetry aspect. When the geometric Langlands construction reduces the four-dimensional 𝒩 = 4 gauge theory on Σ × ℝ² to a two-dimensional sigma-model on the Riemann surface Σ (the Kapustin–Witten compactification [12]), the angle-preserving symmetry of the McGucken-Sphere wavefront descends to the conformal symmetry of the two-dimensional worldsheet — the Virasoro symmetry that organizes the vertex operator algebra of Branch 3. This is the geometric content behind the Five-Aspect assignment Branch 3 ← the spherical-symmetry aspect of §5.

Second, the **single-complex-structure feature**: the perpendicularity of the expanding fourth axis (the imaginary-character aspect) equips ℳ_G with a single complex structure J, J² = −𝟙, in the precise sense of Theorem 5.6 of [3]. Every i that appears downstream — in the connection form A = iH of Branch 1, in the de Rham complex structure of the Hitchin moduli of Branch 4, in the gauge-theoretic phase of Branches 5 and 7 — is one and the same J. The hyperkähler triple (I, J, K) of Branch 4 is the quaternionic enrichment that the McGucken-Sphere geometry forces, with the one-parameter evolution (the active expansion in t) selecting a single distinguished complex structure from the triple per the Stone-theorem collapse of Lemma 12.2 of [3]; its three members are an enrichment of the one J rather than three independent imaginary units competing with it. The de Rham/Dolbeault distinction of non-abelian Hodge theory is the mathematical name for this selection.


## §2.4. The §15.7 Single-i-Engine Identification of [3] — The Foundational Existing Result the Present Paper Extends


The structural-foundational result of [3] that the present paper extends is **Theorem 15.7 of [3]**, the "geometric Langlands parallel to Donaldson" result. The theorem is recalled here in its canonical form.

**(Theorem 15.7 of [3], the geometric Langlands parallel to Donaldson, at grade.)** *(a) [Apparatus, G1/G2] Geometric Langlands is built on flat connections, D-modules, and the Hitchin system — the connection–curvature apparatus carrying the single i (Propositions 15.1–15.3 of [3]). (b) [Chronology, G1] That apparatus was load-bearing in the 1980s–90s, two decades before Kapustin–Witten (2006) identified it as S-duality of 𝒩 = 4 super-Yang–Mills theory (Propositions 15.4, 15.5 of [3]). (c) [Sociology, G1/G2] The delay is the §13.9 pattern of [3]: physics named as pure mathematics, the physical identity recognized late and received as a surprising external bridge (Proposition 15.6 of [3]). (d) [The single i, G2] By Theorem 5.6 of [3] the imaginary unit i in the D-modules, flat connections, Hitchin de Rham structure, and Kapustin–Witten gauge theory is one and the same complex structure — the relativistic i of the active expansion dx₄/dt = ic — developed further, not a distinct i per construction (origin-by-date fallacy, §15.1 of [3], resisted).*

Theorem 15.7 of [3] identifies one foundational object — the connection apparatus — as the single-i engine of the geometric Langlands construction. The present paper extends this identification from the connection apparatus to the seven continuous-geometric branches of mathematics that the geometric Langlands programme bridges. The extension proceeds in §3–§7 below: §3 enumerates the eight branches with explicit defining data, §4 classifies seven as continuous-geometric shadows and one as the discrete-arithmetic shadow, §5 maps the seven continuous-geometric branches to the five aspects of dx₄/dt = ic, §6 documents the sociology-of-absorption pattern across the seven branches, and §7 establishes the arithmetic core as the discrete-arithmetic shadow.

The arithmetic Galois–automorphic core (Langlands 1967), treated in the earlier corpus as an i-free boundary, is here established more precisely as the discrete-arithmetic shadow of the principle — generated through the quantization dx₄/dt = ic produces, the integers being the counts of quanta — with the function-field / number-field analogy of Weil 1940 as the seam between the discrete-arithmetic and continuous-geometric shadow-routes per Theorem 7.1 of §7 below.


## §2.5. Two Load-Bearing Lemmas of [3] Recalled Verbatim — The Single Complex Structure and the Stone-Theorem Collapse


Two structural-foundational results of [3] are invoked repeatedly in the proofs of §4 and §5 below — most prominently in the single-i identification of every connection-form i (Step 1 of Theorem 4.1), in the selection of the de Rham complex structure from the hyperkähler triple (Step 4 of Theorem 4.1 and aspect-association (δ) of Theorem 5.1), and in the origin-by-date-fallacy resistance of §6.2. Because they carry the technical weight of the phrase "one and the same single i" on which the entire continuous-geometric reading rests, they are recalled here in their canonical form so that the downstream proofs operate on stated rather than merely cited results. Their original derivations are in [3]; nothing in the present paper re-derives them, and the recall is exact.

**(Theorem 5.6 of [3], the Single-Complex-Structure Theorem, recalled.)** *The imaginary-character aspect of dx₄/dt = ic equips the McGucken Manifold ℳ_G with a single distinguished complex structure: an endomorphism J of the tangent bundle satisfying J² = −𝟙, given pointwise by the ninety-degree rotation that carries the spatial three-slice (x₁, x₂, x₃) into the expanding fourth axis x₄. Every imaginary unit appearing in the algebraic-shadow content of the principle — the i of the anti-Hermitian connection form A = iH (Branch 1), the i of the canonical commutator [q̂, p̂] = iℏ, the i of the de Rham differential structure of the Hitchin moduli (Branch 4), the i of the Kapustin–Witten gauge-theoretic phase, and the (i/2π) of the Chern–Weil normalization (Branch 7) — is the action of this one J in the relevant representation, not an independently posited imaginary unit that happens to satisfy the same quadratic relation. The identification is forced: two distinct complex structures both arising as the perpendicular advance of one and the same expanding fourth axis at one and the same event coincide, there being only one perpendicular direction to the spatial three-slice up to the orientation fixed by the orientation aspect.*

**(Lemma 12.2 of [3], the Stone-Theorem One-Parameter Collapse, recalled.)** *Let (I, J, K) be a hyperkähler triple of complex structures (I² = J² = K² = IJK = −𝟙) on a manifold carrying a one-parameter evolution generated by a single self-adjoint operator — in the McGucken setting, the evolution in the active-expansion parameter t generated by the McGucken Operator content of dx₄/dt = ic. By Stone's theorem the one-parameter unitary group t ↦ exp(tA) has a single self-adjoint generator A, whose spectral i singles out one distinguished complex structure from the two-sphere {aI + bJ + cK : a² + b² + c² = 1} of complex structures the triple supports: the complex structure in whose holomorphic frame the evolution is diagonal. The selected structure is the de Rham complex structure J of the non-abelian Hodge correspondence; the remaining members of the triple are not eliminated but are not the structure in which the one-parameter active expansion is diagonalized. The selection is therefore not a choice but a consequence of there being a single one-parameter active expansion.*

These two results are used jointly below in exactly one way: wherever a hyperkähler triple, a pair of a-priori-distinct imaginary units, or a "different i per construction" might be supposed, Theorem 5.6 collapses the apparent multiplicity of imaginary units to the one J of the imaginary-character aspect, and Lemma 12.2 collapses the apparent multiplicity of complex structures in a hyperkähler triple to the one de Rham J selected by the one-parameter active expansion. The "single i" of the paper's central claim is, in every instance, the J of Theorem 5.6 acting in a representation, with Lemma 12.2 supplying the selection wherever a triple is present.
